CLASS SCHEDULE WITH TOPICS

Part 1: AI Foundations and Neural Architectures
Day 1 (30 July 2026): AI Basics, Perceive-Reason-Act framework, Three Ingredients of AI, Neural Network Black Box
Day 2 (31 July 2026): Training vs Inference, 5 Eras of AI, Operations Case Study (email generator), Types of AI
Day 3 (3 August 2026): Deconstructing LLMs, Factors of Intelligence, End-to-End LLM Pipeline
Day 4 (4 August 2026): Deterministic vs Probabilistic, Human vs AI Learning Parallels, Pre-Training Data Cleaning Pipeline
Day 5 (5 August 2026): Tokenization Mechanics, Token IDs vs Embeddings, Vector Space Math and Cosine Similarity
Day 6 (6 August 2026): Prompt Caching, 96 Hidden Layers breakdown, Forward Pass, Complete Training Loop

Part 2: AI Engineering, Security and Testing Terminology
Day 7 (7 August 2026): Vocabulary Category 1 - Quality and Risk: Hallucination, Bias, Fairness, Toxicity and Safety, Grounding vs Faithfulness
Day 8 (10 August 2026): Vocabulary Categories 2 and 3 - Mechanics and Security: Context Window, Sampling Controls, RAG vs Fine-Tuning, Prompt Injection, Jailbreaking, Guardrails
Day 9 (11 August 2026): Vocabulary Categories 4 and 5 - Agentic AI and Evals: Agents, Tool and Function Calling, ReAct Loop, Planning, Memory, Reflection, Orchestration, Human in the Loop, Evals, Ground Truth, Golden Data Sets

Part 3: Prompt and Context Engineering
Day 10 (13 August 2026): Prompt Engineering Foundations, Anatomy of a Great Prompt (6 Building Blocks), Prompt Types
Day 11 (14 August 2026): Delimiters, Prompt Parameters (Temperature, Top P, Top K, Max Tokens, Frequency and Presence Penalty, Stop Sequences), System Factors
Day 12 (17 August 2026): Advanced Prompting Techniques (Zero-shot, Few-shot, Chain of Thought, Role and Persona, Chaining and Decomposition), Prompt Frameworks (RTF, CARE, CO-STAR)
Day 13 (18 August 2026): Context Engineering vs Prompt Engineering, Components of Context, Context Budget Management

Part 4: Hands-on Software and Python Programming
Day 14 (19 August 2026): Full-Stack Software Setup (Python 3.12, Node.js, Postman, VS Code, Git)
Day 15 (21 August 2026): Python Basics (Conditions, Loops, Indentation Rules), Data Structure 1: Lists
Day 16 (24 August 2026): Python Data Structures 2 to 4 (Tuples, Sets, Dictionaries)
Day 17 (25 August 2026): Python Functions, Classes, Objects, Constructors (__init__)
Day 18 (26 August 2026): Object-Oriented Programming Pillar 1: Encapsulation (Private attributes, Getters and Setters)
Day 19 (28 August 2026): OOP Pillars 2 to 4 (Inheritance, Polymorphism, Abstraction), Text File Handling

Part 5: RAG and Advanced Topics
Day 20 (9 September 2026): RAG Deep Dive, Chunking Strategies, LangChain RAG pipeline, Student Demo Topics

DAY-BY-DAY SESSION NOTES

Day 1 (30 July 2026): Introductions, AI Fundamentals, Cat vs Dog Example
- Welcome: Satya waited a few minutes for late joiners and introduced himself: about 11 years in IT, the last 3 to 4 years working with AI. First 3 days are warm-up classes.
- 20 student introductions with backgrounds ranging from CSE graduates to 15-year MNC veterans.
- What is AI: Satya explained 3-step loop: Perceive (sensors), Reason (patterns), Act (output).
- Cat vs dog: Vardhan asked how AI distinguishes them. Satya: AI sees millions of labeled images, learns unique features like cat ears vs dog snouts.
- Web data: Companies pay 5-10 lakh rupees for code repositories to train models.
- Building LLMs: AI engineers build on foundation models, not create base LLMs.
- Hidden layers: They perform internal math invisible to users.

Day 2 (31 July 2026): Operations Case Study, A/B Testing, ChatGPT vs Claude
- UK fintech: AI agents reduced operations team from 250 to 70-80 people.
- Email processing: Humans take 15-30 minutes per email. Satya's solution uses 2 LLMs and Salesforce API.
- A/B testing: Route 10% to AI, 90% to humans, gradually increase to 100%.
- LLM preference: ChatGPT for general use, Claude for coding. Satya uses Claude Haiku 4.5 plus GPT-mini.
- Common sense vs patterns: Humans say elephant doesn't fit in fridge. AI checks trained patterns.

Day 3 (3 August 2026): Student Takeaways, Riva Launch, LLM Pipeline Analogy
- Hariprasad M shared TEDx AI insights. Another tested ChatGPT's knowledge cutoff (August 2025).
- Riva announced: 24/7 AI mentor agent using RAG with daily credits for prompt engineering practice.
- Food delivery analogy: Order, add items, pay, cook, package, deliver = prompt, tokenizer, embeddings, transformer, probabilities, output token.

Day 4 (4 August 2026): Data Cleaning and Learning Parallels
- Embedding models: Word2Vec, FastText, Sentence-BERT, Universal Sentence Encoder, Doc2Vec, Hugging Face.
- Human vs AI learning: Baby (pre-training), school (supervised), college (fine-tuning), experience (RLHF).
- Web crawl: Filter language, deduplicate, SEO quality, safety. Result: 10% clean corpus.

Day 5 (5 August 2026): Tokenization and Vector Math
- Tokenization: "tokenization makes LLMs unstoppable" becomes token, ization, makes, LL, M, un, stop, able, period.
- Strawberry question: Balaga Yamini asked why "strawberry" becomes "straw" and "berry." Answer: tokenizer frequency in training data.
- Vector distance: Hariprasad M learned cosine similarity measures angles between vectors.

Day 6 (6 August 2026): Prompt Caching and Hidden Layer Hospital Analogy
- Prompt caching: Hariprasad M asked about caching. Satya explained that cache means temporary memory and that LLM companies store frequent tokens in a caching database, and said he would cover it in more depth later.
- Hospital analogy: 96 layers = receptionist (input), nurse (symptoms), doctor (diagnosis), pharmacist (medicine).
- Vectors in layers: B Sujay and Joshna learned vectors stay vectorized across all layers, only decode at output.

Days 7-8 (7 and 10 August 2026): Quality, Risk, Security
- Hallucination, bias, fairness, toxicity: Satya and students discussed quality metrics and safety filters.
- Grounding vs faithfulness: Vardhan learned to cite sources. HR policy says 10 leaves, not 12 or 15.
- Prompt injection vs jailbreaking: System override vs safety bypass.
- Sampling: Sanjay Krishna learned temperature (0.1 vs 1.2), Top K, Top P.

Days 9-10 (11 and 13 August 2026): Agentic AI and System Prompts
- Agents, tool calling, ReAct loops, planning, memory, HITL: Core agentic concepts.
- System prompt: Satya's 160-line production prompt for interview tool and Riva covering role, format, STAR rules, length controls.
- 6 building blocks: role, context, task, format, constraints, examples. Most prompts use 3-4.
- Weak vs strong: "Write about agents" vs structured 5-part prompt showed massive differences.

Days 11-12 (14 and 17 August 2026): Delimiters and Email Exercises
- Lloyd's Bank email: Hariprasad M shared real privacy email. Satya showed wrapping text in triple quotes or braces.
- CTC exercise: Satya used Chain of Thought for step-by-step salary calculations.
- Prompt caching savings (Day 11, 14 August 2026): Satya showed he saved 13 dollars in 7 days using prompt caching on Anthropic, on 13.8 million tokens used only by the interview tool. Hariprasad M answered "13 dollars" when Satya asked how much he saved, and Balaga Vishalakshi reacted with surprise.

Days 13-14 (18 and 19 August 2026): Context Pruning and Setup
- Context pruning: Satya cut history from 40 questions to 5 minutes for speed and tokens.
- Setup: Terminal checks (python, pip, node, git) with Srinivas, Vamshi, Karthik, Bindu.

Days 15-19 (21-28 August 2026): Python, OOP, Hands-on Coding
- Factorial review, tuple immutability, character counting, longest substring, encapsulation, inheritance, polymorphism.
- Real-world code issues and fixes demonstrated live.

Day 20 (9 September 2026): RAG Deep Dive and Demos
- Plain LLM vs RAG: Generic 30-day policy vs actual 14-day policy from March 2026 with source citations.
- Chunking: fixed-size, recursive character (400-600 chars, 100 overlap), semantic, document-aware.
- LangChain 4-step: PyPDFLoader, RecursiveCharacterTextSplitter, Chroma.from_documents, as_retriever.
- Demos: Hariprasad M (embeddings), Joshna (chunking), Mahesh Y (RAG testing).
